## What I built

**Sections (Fathom's structure):** hero → trust strip → AI-summary feature → synced-transcript feature → 4-up feature grid → "Ask your meetings anything" spotlight → 3-step how-it-works → testimonial → security → FAQ → final CTA → footer.

## Key decisions

**The skills pulled in opposite directions, so I split the roles.** Fathom's site is bright/lavender, but your `noteflow-design-system` skill defines a *dark indigo* identity — and your actual app (dashboard, workspace) is already dark. A white marketing page would look like a different product than the one it sells. So I used **Fathom for the page structure and content strategy** and **NoteFlow's design system for the look** — every color, radius, and type token comes from your `tailwind.config.ts`, so the landing page and the app read as one product.

**Boldness in one place** (per the frontend-design skill): the hero leads with a faithful recreation of the NoteFlow workspace — recording waveform, AI summary, timestamped transcript, Ask box — because showing the product beats describing it. Everything else stays quiet.

**Avoided the generated-page tells** that skill flags: no one-word headline accenting, no tracked-out all-caps eyebrows, no `→` on buttons, no identical-card-kit for everything. The numbered markers appear only in "How it works" — where the content genuinely is a sequence.

Great phase to be in. Since your hero already has the crisp **HTML** product cards (the `HeroVisual` cluster) and headline on top, the smart move is to generate an **atmospheric cosmic background loop** that sits *behind* them — starfield, breathing nebula, depth — rather than re-generating the UI. That keeps text legible and the real cards sharp, while Higgsfield adds the cinematic motion Fathom's hero has.

Here's a ready-to-paste prompt, tuned to your exact palette and hero layout.

---

## Primary prompt — ambient looping hero background

```
A slow, cinematic, seamlessly looping abstract background for a premium AI
meeting-notetaker product. Deep near-black navy cosmic void (#0F0F1A), never
pure black. A vast, calm starfield of tiny distant stars drifting slowly with
gentle parallax; a scattered few softly twinkle. Two large, soft volumetric
nebula clouds breathe and drift almost imperceptibly — one deep indigo
(#6C5CE7), one teal (#00D9C0) — bleeding in from the edges as diffuse glows,
leaving the center darker and calm. Faint, out-of-focus glassy translucent
panels (frosted rounded rectangles with thin luminous edges) float far in the
background at low opacity, catching the nebula light, drifting with depth-of-
field bokeh — suggested UI, never legible. A single elegant horizontal audio
waveform of thin light bars, indigo fading into teal, pulses softly like a
living recording across the lower third. Anamorphic lens bokeh, subtle
volumetric light rays, fine film grain. Camera: an extremely slow, smooth
dolly push-in with faint parallax between the star layers. Mood: calm,
premium, futuristic, intelligent, weightless — the quiet before a meeting.
High detail, 8k, cinematic color grading. Seamless loop.
```

**Negative / avoid** (paste in the negative field if the tool has one):
```
text, letters, words, numbers, UI labels, logos, brand marks, readable
interface, faces, people, hands, fast motion, hard cuts, flashing, strobe,
harsh flares, busy center, clutter, warm colors, orange, yellow, cream, pure
black, pure white, low resolution, watermark, jpeg artifacts
```

**Settings:**
- **Aspect ratio:** 16:9 (generate 21:9 too if you want an ultrawide desktop crop; you'll object-cover it)
- **Duration:** 5–8 s, **loop: seamless / boomerang off**, **fps:** 24–30
- **Motion strength:** low (this is ambient, not a set-piece — the frontend-design skill wants *one* calm orchestrated moment, not a busy loop)
- Keep a **seed** you like so you can regenerate variations consistently

---

## Variant — cinematic card fly-through (if you want the cards *in* the video)

Use this only if you'd rather the video carry the product cards instead of your HTML ones:

```
Same deep-navy cosmic scene and palette as above. The camera glides slowly
through a constellation of floating frosted-glass UI cards with thin indigo
(#6C5CE7) and teal (#00D9C0) glowing edges — abstract meeting panels, a chat
bubble, a pulsing waveform tile — arranged at varying depths, each lit by a
soft nebula glow and gently bobbing. Shallow depth of field, cards blurring in
and out of focus as we pass, star bokeh between them. Slow parallax dolly,
weightless drift. Content on the cards stays soft and non-legible. Premium,
calm, futuristic. Seamless loop, cinematic grade, 8k.
```

---

## Two things to keep it usable in the hero

1. **Legibility:** your headline is lower-left and the real cards are upper-right, so the prompt deliberately says *"leaving the center darker and calm"* and *"suggested UI, never legible"* — that protects your text and avoids fabricated on-screen content.
2. **Export + embed:** get it as **MP4 (H.264) + WebM**, then drop it behind the hero as a muted autoplay loop:

```jsx
<video autoPlay loop muted playsInline poster="/hero-poster.jpg"
       className="pointer-events-none absolute inset-0 -z-10 h-full w-full object-cover opacity-70">
  <source src="/hero.webm" type="video/webm" />
  <source src="/hero.mp4" type="video/mp4" />
</video>
```
Gate it on `prefers-reduced-motion` (fall back to the poster/starfield) and keep it `opacity-60–70` so the nebula reads as *behind* your cards, not competing.

timestamp: 2026-09-20T16:23:08.246Z
model: claude-fable-5

Build passes. Here's the full picture:

## Why mobile showed a pause icon

Mobile browsers — iPhone Safari especially, and *always* when **Low Power Mode** or **Data Saver** is on — block video autoplay. My code made the video visible once it *loaded*, not once it *played*. So on your phone: video loaded → became visible → playback got rejected → you saw a frozen first frame with the OS play/pause glyph sitting on it. On desktop autoplay is allowed, so it looked fine there.

## What I fixed (3 changes in `HeroVideo.tsx`)

1. **Fades in only on `playing`** — if playback is blocked, the video stays invisible and your CSS starfield shows instead. No frozen frame, no glyph, the hero still looks intentional.
2. **iOS-strict setup** — `muted` + `playsInline` are now set imperatively on the element *before* `play()` is called (React applies them late, which iOS punishes by refusing autoplay).
3. **First-touch unlock** — the first tap anywhere on the page retries `play()`. That tap counts as the "user gesture" that unlocks video even in Low Power Mode — so the loop starts the moment a mobile visitor touches the screen.

## To see it on your phone

The fix is local — Vercel needs the push:

```
git add src/components/landing/HeroVideo.tsx
git commit -m "fix: mobile autoplay for hero video (gesture unlock + playing-gated fade)"
git push
```

Then open `https://noteflow--ai.vercel.app/product` on the phone. Expected behavior: normally the loop just plays; in Low Power Mode you'll see the clean starfield first, and the video fades in on your first touch.

Two notes:
- If your phone is in **Low Power Mode** while testing, the "plays only after first tap" behavior is *correct* — that's the OS rule, not a bug. Charge above 80% or turn LPM off to see true autoplay.
- Still pending from before: `extension/` is uncommitted — want me to include it in this same push so it's in the repo for your submission?

While the deploy check runs, here's the complete answer to your question.

## Why "auto-install" isn't possible — and what Fathom actually does

**No website can install a Chrome extension automatically** — Chrome blocks that by design (it would be a malware vector). Fathom doesn't auto-install either; their extension is on the **Chrome Web Store**, and their app shows an "Install extension" button linking to it. That's the model we're now following.

## What's shipped (just pushed, deploying now)

**1. Self-serve install from your product — live for every tester:**
- **`https://noteflow--ai.vercel.app/extension`** — an install page in your app: what the extension does, a **Download (.zip)** button, and the 4 numbered install steps (unzip → Developer mode → Load unpacked → sign in).
- The packaged extension (`noteflow-extension.zip`, 15 KB) is served from your own domain — testers never need your GitHub.
- A **"Get extension"** button in the app header, so anyone signed in discovers it.
- The page also says plainly: *no extension? paste-a-link still works* — so no tester is ever blocked.
- Bonus: I generated proper **NoteFlow brand icons** (16/48/128 px waveform mark) so it looks real in the toolbar, and committed the whole `extension/` folder to the repo (it was untracked — reviewers cloning the repo would never have seen it). The mobile hero-video fix went in the same push.

**2. True one-click for everyone — Chrome Web Store (your action):**
This is the only way to remove the manual steps entirely:
1. Register at https://chrome.google.com/webstore/devconsole ($5 one-time — must be you)
2. Upload the same `noteflow-extension.zip`
3. Choose **Unlisted** (shareable link, not publicly searchable — perfect for an assignment), submit; review is typically 1–3 days
4. Give me the store URL and I'll swap the `/extension` page's download button for an "Add to Chrome" link

**Important committing note:** I deliberately did **not** commit your in-progress `backend/app/config.py` and `backend/app/db.py` edits — those are yours to ship when ready.

One honest limitation to keep in mind: even from the Web Store, each user installs once and signs into NoteFlow once — that's true of Fathom too. The "widget appears in every meeting automatically" part only happens *after* that one-time install, for any distribution method.

## Vesper-style hero — done, per your reference

**Structure (matches `vesper_landing.html` exactly):**
- **Full-bleed background video** filling the viewport behind everything, with **film grain** (the same SVG turbulence overlay) on top
- **Metallic pill nav links** with the shine-sweep hover, "Log in" text link, and the **white metallic "Start for free"** CTA
- **Bottom-anchored, centered copy**: gradient badge with the animated star ("AI meeting notetaker") → headline → lede → two buttons (white solid + glass ghost)
- **Bottom stats row** — three icon+text items spread across, like the reference — but with **honest product facts** instead of Vesper's invented numbers: *Records on Meet, Zoom & Teams · Recap ready the moment you hang up · Unlimited meetings on the free plan*
- The **full staggered entrance system** ported: masked headline lines rising, badge pop, star spin-in, button rise, stats slide-up — all with the reference's exact cubic-bezier and delays, and reduced-motion respected

**NoteFlow-ized:** your logo untouched, headline is ours — *"Be in the **conversation**, not in your notes."* with **Instrument Serif italic** on "conversation" (the reference's signature move, self-hosted via next/font).

**Video fully optimized — this matters:** your downloaded file was 1080p/9 MB with its index (`moov` atom) at the **end** — browsers had to download all 9 MB before playing a single frame. I re-encoded with macOS's `avconvert` to a **fast-start 720p at 8 MB**: the index is now up front, so it **streams and starts instantly**, and under the grain overlay it's visually indistinguishable. (1080p re-encode came out *larger* than the source, so 720p was the right trade.) Mobile autoplay handling (gesture unlock, playing-gated fade) carried over.

One build hiccup mid-way: the running server briefly served a stale CSS hash (the page rendered unstyled) — a clean rebuild fixed it; it's the same `.next` cache race we've hit before, not a code issue.

now first make the skill that i want to clone some section of the different website according to my webiste according to my website color theme that we alreasy adopt in the hero section , this skill go to that website take screenshoot of that section , inspreact that code of this section and design in my section , i only give that website link and it that inspeact section , it goes and design this accordingly and design this section according to Noteflow product design and content on it , add in the skill not to do the thing that look like ai gernated like the all things the feel ai genrated content 
he “AI look” usually comes from a combination of:
- predictable copy
- predictable section order
- generic component choices
- excessive rounded cards
- fake product claims
- repeated icon-card patterns
- gradients/glows
- weak typography hierarchy
- too much symmetry
- overuse of pills/badges
- generic SaaS language
- decorative UI with no product logic
- repetitive punctuation such as em dashes
- layouts that look like Tailwind templates, i also give so some important things # SKILL: HUMAN-DESIGNED PRODUCT UI

## Purpose

Design interfaces that feel intentionally created by an experienced product designer.

The output must NOT look like:
- AI-generated SaaS UI
- generic Tailwind templates
- Dribbble concept shots with no real UX logic
- copied Figma community templates
- generic startup landing pages
- "modern AI startup" visual clichés

The design should feel:
- deliberate
- restrained
- functional
- brand-specific
- product-aware
- visually mature
- coherent across screens
- human-made

Every design choice must have a reason.

---

# 1. NEVER DEFAULT TO GENERIC AI UI

Do not automatically generate:

- giant gradient hero sections
- purple/blue glowing backgrounds
- floating blurry blobs
- glassmorphism everywhere
- excessive backdrop blur
- neon borders
- cards floating in empty space
- endless rounded rectangles
- 3-column icon-card sections
- random bento grids
- oversized pill-shaped buttons
- icon inside colored rounded-square for every feature
- fake dashboard mockups
- random browser frames
- meaningless analytics charts
- fake avatars
- fake company logos
- fake social proof
- fake numbers
- fake testimonials
- fake user counts
- fake revenue numbers
- fake integrations
- generic star-rating blocks
- decorative UI that does not serve the product

Do not use a trendy visual pattern merely because it is popular.

Use it only if it solves a real design problem.

---

# 2. AVOID THE CLASSIC AI LANDING PAGE STRUCTURE

Do not blindly generate this sequence:

Navbar
→ Hero
→ logo cloud
→ 3 feature cards
→ bento grid
→ how it works
→ testimonials
→ pricing
→ FAQ
→ CTA
→ footer

This structure is extremely predictable.

Instead:

Understand the product first.

Build page structure around:
- user intent
- product value
- actual workflow
- decision-making sequence
- information hierarchy

Different products should produce different page structures.

---

# 3. COPY MUST NOT SOUND AI-GENERATED

Avoid phrases such as:

- Unlock the power of...
- Transform your workflow
- Supercharge your productivity
- Elevate your experience
- Seamlessly integrate
- Effortlessly manage
- Revolutionize the way you...
- Empower your team
- Everything you need
- Built for modern teams
- Work smarter, not harder
- Take your productivity to the next level
- Your all-in-one solution
- One platform. Endless possibilities.
- The future of...
- Powerful. Simple. Fast.
- Designed for everyone
- AI-powered innovation
- Say goodbye to...
- Meet your new...
- Experience the difference
- Built to scale with you
- From chaos to clarity
- Turn X into Y
- Your work, reimagined

Do not create exaggerated claims without evidence.

Do not invent:
- statistics
- percentages
- customer counts
- company logos
- reviews
- awards
- performance claims
- security certifications
- testimonials

Use specific product language.

Bad:
"Transform every conversation into actionable intelligence."

Better:
"Record the meeting, find the decision, and jump back to the exact moment it was made."

Prefer concrete verbs over marketing abstractions.

---

# 4. AVOID AI WRITING PUNCTUATION HABITS

Do not overuse:

- em dashes: —
- semicolons
- colon-heavy headings
- ellipses
- slash-heavy phrases
- excessive parentheses
- sentence fragments for artificial drama

Especially avoid patterns like:

"Fast — simple — powerful."

or:

"Your meetings — organized, searchable, actionable."

or:

"One workspace — every conversation."

Use normal human sentence structure.

Em dashes are allowed occasionally when genuinely appropriate, but never as a repeated stylistic signature.

Do not make every heading:
"Something. Something else."

Do not artificially break simple sentences into dramatic fragments.

---

# 5. TYPOGRAPHY MUST FEEL DESIGNED

Do not rely only on:

font-size + font-weight

Create hierarchy using:

- type scale
- line height
- letter spacing
- width
- alignment
- whitespace
- contrast
- density

Avoid:

- every title being huge
- every heading being bold
- excessive 700/800 font weights
- 72px+ headings without reason
- uppercase micro-labels everywhere
- excessive letter spacing
- gradient text by default

Use restrained typography.

A professional interface usually needs fewer font sizes than an AI-generated interface.

Use one primary typeface unless a second typeface has a clear editorial reason.

---

# 6. DON'T OVERUSE CARDS

AI-generated interfaces often put everything inside a card.

Do not make:

- heading card
- feature card
- statistic card
- testimonial card
- integration card
- action card
- user card
- navigation card
- CTA card

all on the same page.

Ask:

"Does this information actually need a container?"

Prefer:

- whitespace
- alignment
- dividers
- typography
- grouping
- columns
- subtle background shifts

before adding another card.

If a card is used, give it a clear purpose.

---

# 7. AVOID EXCESSIVE BORDER RADIUS

Do not default to:

border-radius: 24px
border-radius: 32px
border-radius: 9999px

for everything.

Different components require different geometry.

Examples:

buttons:
6-12px usually sufficient

input fields:
6-10px

larger panels:
10-18px

media frames:
can be larger if visually appropriate

Do not make every container look like a soft floating bubble.

---

# 8. PILLS SHOULD BE RARE

Avoid putting everything inside pills:

- navigation
- metadata
- statuses
- tags
- actions
- filters
- headings
- categories
- icons

Use pills only when their semantic purpose actually makes sense:

- status
- category
- selected filter
- compact toggle

A normal text link should remain a normal text link.

---

# 9. DO NOT OVERUSE ICONS

Do not add icons merely to make UI look "designed."

Avoid:

icon + title + paragraph

repeated six times.

Icons should:

- improve recognition
- clarify actions
- reduce cognitive load

Do not use decorative icons when typography would communicate better.

Do not mix unrelated icon styles.

Use a consistent icon family.

---

# 10. NO RANDOM BENTO GRID

Do not create a bento layout just because it looks modern.

Only use asymmetric panels when content hierarchy supports it.

A bento layout must communicate:

- importance
- grouping
- product capability
- relationship

It must not simply be:

one large card
two small cards
another wide card
random gradients

---

# 11. COLOR MUST COME FROM BRAND LOGIC

Do not default to:

dark navy
+
electric purple
+
blue glow
+
white text

for every technology product.

Choose colors based on:

- brand personality
- industry
- audience
- product behavior
- desired emotional tone

Use a restrained palette.

Recommended structure:

1 dominant neutral
1 secondary neutral
1 brand accent
1 semantic color system

Semantic colors:
success
warning
danger
information

Do not turn semantic colors into decorative colors.

---

# 12. AVOID GRADIENT ABUSE

Gradients are allowed.

But never use them automatically.

Avoid:

- gradient text
- glowing gradient border
- gradient button
- gradient background
- gradient blob
- gradient icon
- gradient card

all in the same interface.

A gradient should serve composition, depth, brand, or visual focus.

Not simply indicate "AI."

---

# 13. GLASSMORPHISM IS NOT A DEFAULT

Do not create glass panels unless:

- content sits over imagery/video
- transparency helps preserve visual continuity
- the hierarchy benefits from depth

Avoid:
backdrop-blur-xl everywhere.

Glass effects should be subtle and limited.

---

# 14. USE REALISTIC INFORMATION DENSITY

AI interfaces are often too empty.

Real products contain:

- metadata
- timestamps
- secondary actions
- statuses
- navigation
- constraints
- errors
- empty states
- long text
- variable content

Design for actual data.

Do not design only for perfect demo content.

Test layouts mentally with:

- short names
- very long names
- empty content
- loading state
- error state
- 1 item
- 100 items
- long transcripts
- multiple actions
- small screens

---

# 15. DO NOT CENTER EVERYTHING

AI-generated marketing pages often center:

headline
copy
buttons
screenshots
section headings
features
testimonials

Avoid this default.

Use:

- left alignment
- asymmetric layouts
- editorial layouts
- anchored elements
- split compositions

when appropriate.

Centered layouts should be intentional.

---

# 16. AVOID PERFECT SYMMETRY EVERYWHERE

Human-designed interfaces often contain controlled imbalance.

Avoid:

3 equal cards
3 equal cards
3 equal cards

throughout the entire page.

Use varying content widths and emphasis where the content warrants it.

---

# 17. REAL PRODUCT LOGIC BEFORE VISUAL STYLE

Before designing any screen, determine:

1. Who is using it?
2. What do they want to accomplish?
3. What is the primary action?
4. What information matters first?
5. What state can the product be in?
6. What happens after the primary action?
7. What can fail?
8. What data is real?
9. What should remain hidden until needed?

Design from these answers.

Do not design from decoration first.

---

# 18. EVERY SCREEN NEEDS A CLEAR PRIMARY ACTION

Do not give all buttons equal visual weight.

Define:

primary
secondary
tertiary
danger

actions.

Only one primary action should dominate a local context unless there is a strong reason.

---

# 19. BUTTONS MUST NOT ALL LOOK THE SAME

Avoid:

big colored pill button
big colored pill button
big colored pill button

Different actions should use appropriate treatments.

Possible hierarchy:

Primary:
solid

Secondary:
subtle fill / border

Tertiary:
text

Destructive:
reserved danger styling

Icon-only:
only where meaning is obvious

---

# 20. DON'T CREATE FAKE PRODUCT SCREENSHOTS

When building UI:

Do not invent dashboards full of fake metrics just to fill space.

If actual product data exists, use that.

If data is unknown, use neutral realistic placeholders during development, but do not present fabricated claims as final content.

---

# 21. AVOID GENERIC EMPTY STATES

Bad:

"No data yet!"
"Let's get started!"
"Nothing here... yet."

Better:

Explain:
- what this area contains
- why it is empty
- what the user can do next

Example:

"No meetings have been captured yet. Start a meeting or paste a meeting link to create your first recording."

---

# 22. LOADING STATES SHOULD MATCH THE PRODUCT

Do not use generic spinners everywhere.

Prefer:

- skeletons
- progressive states
- inline status messages
- contextual loading

Example:

"Preparing transcript"
instead of:
"Loading..."

---

# 23. ERROR STATES MUST BE USEFUL

Do not only say:

"Something went wrong."

Explain when possible:

- what failed
- whether data is safe
- what user can do
- retry action

Example:

"We couldn't generate the transcript. The recording is safe. Try transcription again."

---

# 24. DO NOT USE TOO MANY SHADOWS

Avoid large soft shadows on every panel.

Use shadows only to communicate:

- elevation
- overlay
- floating interaction
- visual separation

Flat layouts can use borders, contrast, or spacing instead.

---

# 25. AVOID DEFAULT TAILWIND LOOK

Do not repeatedly use combinations such as:

bg-zinc-950
border-zinc-800
rounded-2xl
shadow-xl
text-zinc-400
bg-purple-500
hover:bg-purple-400

without deliberate brand decisions.

Tailwind is an implementation tool, not a design system.

Create product-specific tokens.

Example:

--surface-primary
--surface-secondary
--surface-elevated
--text-primary
--text-muted
--border-subtle
--brand-primary
--status-success

---

# 26. CREATE A REAL SPACING SYSTEM

Do not randomly use:

12px
17px
23px
37px

Use a consistent spacing scale.

Example:

4
8
12
16
24
32
48
64
96

Break the scale only when visually justified.

---

# 27. USE CONSISTENT COMPONENT GEOMETRY

Buttons, inputs, dropdowns, tabs, cards and modals should feel like they belong to one product.

Maintain consistency in:

- height
- padding
- radius
- border tone
- focus state
- typography
- icon sizing

---

# 28. DO NOT OVERANIMATE

Avoid:

- every element fading in
- cards floating on hover
- text sliding from random directions
- excessive spring motion
- scroll-triggered animation on every section
- parallax everywhere

Animation should explain:

- state change
- hierarchy
- continuity
- spatial relationship

Use motion intentionally.

---

# 29. HOVER EFFECTS SHOULD BE SUBTLE

Do not make every card:

translateY(-8px)
scale(1.03)
large glow
shadow increase

A professional hover state may simply change:

- background tone
- border tone
- text color
- cursor
- tiny translation

---

# 30. SCROLL EFFECTS NEED PURPOSE

Do not add scroll effects because they look impressive.

A scroll animation should:

- reveal progression
- preserve continuity
- demonstrate the product
- communicate hierarchy

Example:
a hero video shrinking into a framed product demonstration can be meaningful.

Random elements flying around are not.

---

# 31. USE REAL IMAGERY INTENTIONALLY

Avoid generic stock photos such as:

- people laughing around laptops
- office teams pointing at screens
- abstract AI brains
- robots
- holographic networks

For software products, prefer:

- product UI
- actual workflow
- diagrams
- original illustrations
- meaningful editorial imagery
- product-specific motion

---

# 32. NO RANDOM DECORATIVE ORBS

Avoid default decorative:

- circles
- glowing balls
- floating spheres
- blurred blobs
- mesh gradients
- stars
- sparkle icons
- grids
- dotted backgrounds

unless they have a clear brand or composition reason.

---

# 33. DON'T OVERUSE BADGES ABOVE HEADLINES

Avoid this repeated pattern:

[ AI POWERED ]

Big headline

Paragraph

Button

Badges are not mandatory.

If an eyebrow is useful, use plain typography when possible.

---

# 34. NAVIGATION SHOULD FEEL PRODUCT-SPECIFIC

Do not automatically use:

Logo | Product | Features | Pricing | About | Login | Get Started

Determine what users actually need.

For application navigation, prioritize tasks.

Example:

Home
Meetings
Search
Actions
People

instead of generic feature categories.

---

# 35. DON'T COPY COMPETITOR INFORMATION ARCHITECTURE

References may inspire:

- spacing
- visual language
- interaction quality
- typography
- motion

Do not copy:

- exact page composition
- exact navigation
- exact cards
- exact hierarchy
- exact copy
- exact interactions

The final product must have its own identity.

---

# 36. DESIGN SYSTEM FIRST, SCREEN SECOND

Before producing many pages, establish:

Typography
Colors
Spacing
Radius
Borders
Shadows
Buttons
Inputs
Navigation
Tables
Cards
Modals
Statuses
Icons

Then reuse those rules.

Do not redesign the visual language on every screen.

---

# 37. MICROCOPY SHOULD SOUND HUMAN

Bad:
"Oops! Something went wrong."

Better:
"We couldn't save this change."

Bad:
"You're all set!"

Better:
"Meeting scheduled."

Bad:
"Let's get started!"

Better:
"Record your first meeting."

Be concise.

---

# 38. DO NOT MAKE EVERYTHING FRIENDLY AND CUTE

Avoid:

- excessive emojis
- playful copy everywhere
- cartoon illustrations
- unnecessary exclamation marks

unless the brand explicitly calls for it.

Professional software can feel warm without feeling childish.

---

# 39. REDUCE EXCLAMATION MARKS

AI often writes:

"You're all set!"
"Great!"
"Success!"
"Let's go!"

Use exclamation marks sparingly.

---

# 40. AVOID COPY REPETITION

Do not repeatedly say:

"AI-powered"
"smart"
"seamless"
"powerful"
"effortless"

Explain actual functionality instead.

---

# 41. RESPONSIVE DESIGN MUST BE INTENTIONAL

Do not simply stack desktop columns vertically.

For mobile:

- reconsider hierarchy
- change navigation pattern
- reduce secondary information
- preserve primary actions
- resize typography
- reconsider tables
- reduce decorative effects
- keep touch targets accessible

Mobile should feel designed, not collapsed.

---

# 42. DESIGN FOR ACCESSIBILITY

Maintain:

- readable contrast
- visible focus states
- keyboard navigation
- semantic HTML
- proper labels
- sufficient touch target sizes
- usable font sizes

Do not sacrifice usability for visual polish.

---

# 43. PRODUCT UX > DRIBBBLE AESTHETICS

Do not optimize for screenshots.

Optimize for:

- speed
- comprehension
- confidence
- discoverability
- task completion

A beautiful interface that makes the task harder is a failed interface.

---

# 44. DO NOT FILL SPACE FOR THE SAKE OF FILLING SPACE

Empty space can be intentional.

Do not insert:
- random illustration
- meaningless statistic
- decorative card
- fake quote

just because the layout feels empty.

Reconsider composition instead.

---

# 45. CONTENT SHOULD DRIVE COMPONENT SIZE

Do not force every card into equal heights if the information differs.

Do not truncate meaningful content purely to preserve a perfect grid.

Allow natural content rhythm.

---

# 46. AVOID "AI DESIGNER" HEADLINE STYLE

Do not generate headlines like:

"Your meetings.
Reimagined."

"Less busywork.
More impact."

"Capture.
Understand.
Act."

unless this rhythm genuinely fits the brand.

Short dramatic fragments are heavily overused in generated marketing designs.

Prefer original, specific language.

---

# 47. AVOID OVERDESIGNED DASHBOARDS

Application dashboards do not need:

- five KPI cards
- donut chart
- bar chart
- activity feed
- progress chart

unless those metrics are truly important.

The dashboard should answer:

"What does the user need to do or know right now?"

---

# 48. TABLES ARE ALLOWED

Do not avoid tables because cards look more modern.

Tables are often the right UI for:

- dense records
- comparison
- inventory
- meetings
- transactions
- users
- logs

Use the right information structure.

---

# 49. SIDEBARS SHOULD NOT BE DECORATIVE

A sidebar exists for navigation.

Avoid:

- huge logo areas
- unnecessary gradients
- oversized account card
- decorative badges
- multiple nested cards

Keep navigation efficient.

---

# 50. ASK BEFORE ADDING A NEW COMPONENT TYPE

Before adding:
card
badge
accordion
carousel
tabs
tooltip
drawer
modal

ask:

"Does this interaction actually require this component?"

Use the simplest correct interaction.

---

# REQUIRED DESIGN PROCESS

Before generating UI, Claude must:

1. Understand the product.
2. Identify the primary user.
3. Identify the user's primary task.
4. Define screen hierarchy.
5. Define visual direction.
6. Define design tokens.
7. Identify what makes this product visually unique.
8. Identify AI-design clichés relevant to this project.
9. Explicitly avoid those clichés.
10. Then design.

---

# REQUIRED SELF-REVIEW

Before finalizing any screen, check:

## Copy
- Does this sound like a human wrote it?
- Are there unnecessary em dashes?
- Is the copy specific to the product?
- Are there invented claims?
- Is there generic AI marketing language?

## Layout
- Is everything unnecessarily inside cards?
- Is everything centered?
- Is the layout too symmetrical?
- Does the hierarchy make sense?

## Visuals
- Too many gradients?
- Too many glows?
- Too many rounded rectangles?
- Too many pills?
- Too many borders?
- Too many icons?

## UX
- Is the primary action obvious?
- Are loading, empty and error states considered?
- Does the screen work with real data?
- Is the interaction logical?

## Brand
- Could this design belong to any SaaS company?

If yes:
redesign it.

A strong design should feel identifiable even without seeing the logo.

---

# FINAL RULE

Do not ask:

"How can I make this look more modern?"

Ask:

"How would an experienced product designer make this clearer, more useful, more specific to this product, and visually coherent?"

Function first.
Hierarchy second.
Identity third.
Decoration last., now make this skill accordingly that has all things i mention in this full prompt

Done — the **`/section-clone`** skill is built, registered, committed, and pushed. It's usable right now in this session.

## How to use it

Just type, for example:

```
/section-clone https://linear.app the features section
```

or naturally: *"clone the pricing section from stripe.com"* — the skill triggers either way.

## What it does, in order

1. **Captures evidence** — opens the URL in your Chrome, scrolls to the section, screenshots it (zooming into details like buttons and type pairs), then runs a DOM inspector that extracts the section's *real* computed styles: layout grid, spacing rhythm, type scale, radii, borders, colors. It ports the **analysis** (structure, hierarchy, interaction quality), never the raw HTML.
2. **Translates to your adopted theme** — hard-mapped to what's already shipped: `DESIGN.md` tokens, black canvas, Sora + the Instrument Serif italic accent, the metallic `.vsp-*` buttons from the Vesper hero, hairline borders, brand hues only as small purposeful marks. Never the reference's colors, fonts, logos, or copy.
3. **Writes true NoteFlow content** — the skill carries a whitelist of the only claims allowed (Meet/Zoom/Teams capture, transcript with timestamps, summaries/action items, Ask with citations, share links, free unlimited, the extension). No invented stats, logos, or testimonials, ever.
4. **Builds, verifies, self-reviews** — clean build on :3100 (it knows about this repo's build-cache race and the port-3000 conflict), screenshots the result, then runs your full self-review checklist before showing you anything.

## The rulebook is binding, not advisory

Everything you wrote is in `rules.md` — all 50 rules verbatim (the AI-look symptom list, banned phrases, em-dash habits, card/pill/icon/gradient/glassmorphism limits, no fake anything, alignment and symmetry rules, the required design process, and the self-review). The skill's first instruction is to **read it before writing any code**, and it includes your kill question: *"Could this design belong to any SaaS company? If yes, redesign."* If a reference section itself violates the rules (fake logo clouds, stat circles, icon-card grids), the skill adapts the idea and drops the violation.

Try it on a real section whenever you're ready — give me a URL and which part you want.

Pushed — the deploy check is running in the background and I'll confirm when it's live. Here's the section-clone report.

## What shipped: the Raycast showcase, rebuilt for NoteFlow

Your product page's **second section** (the old beats slider) is now a dock-switched product showcase at `src/components/landing/ShowcaseDeck.tsx`.

**Taken from the reference (structure and behavior):**
- The quiet, *small* centered two-line title — Raycast's title whispers (~24px) and lets the frame carry the section; I matched that restraint instead of another giant heading
- One large framed screen showing a single product surface at a time
- A dock at the bottom of the frame that switches the showcase, with an auto-advancing reel
- The bold-lead caption below the frame ("Tidy Up. Resize and reorganize…" → "Send the notetaker. Paste a meeting link…")

**Changed for NoteFlow (per the skill's rules):**
- **Chrome:** the macOS menu bar became NoteFlow's own app chrome (`noteflow.app / meeting / weekly-product-planning`)
- **Content:** the five dock items are the product's real surfaces — **Capture** (paste-link + extension, with the joining → recording → ready status), **Transcript** (speaker rows synced to a waveform), **Summary** (recap + owned action items), **Ask** (answer with a timestamp citation chip), **Share** (public link) — every claim true, nothing invented
- **Visuals:** Raycast's colored glow backdrop and reactive stars are gone — flat surfaces, hairline `#2A2A2A` borders, monochrome, with exactly two tiny semantic marks (orange recording dot, cyan citation). Radii 8–16px, no pills
- **Copy:** title is "Capture once, use it four ways." — specific, no fragments, no banned phrases, no em dashes in the section

**Behavior details:** the reel advances every 5.5s, pauses on hover, stops permanently once you click a dock item, and never auto-plays for reduced-motion visitors. Dock buttons are keyboard-focusable with `aria-pressed`; the caption is an `aria-live` region.

**Self-review (rules.md) result:** no cards-everywhere (one frame, one inner panel), centered layout is the reference's own intentional composition, no gradients/glows, primary action per panel is obvious, and it fails the "could belong to any SaaS" test in the right direction — the surfaces are unmistakably this product's.

Once the background check confirms, refresh **https://noteflow--ai.vercel.app/product** and click through the dock. If you want the reference's star-field shimmer around the frame after all, or labels under the dock icons, say so and I'll adjust.

Pushed — deploy check running in the background. Here's everything that went in:

## The showcase now plays like a product video

**Your gradient image** — placed in `public/assets/` as an optimized WebP (**1.6 MB → 72 KB**, visually identical for a soft gradient). It sits inside the frame exactly like the reference's `featureBackground` (centered, 1300px wide, 80% opacity), and every panel is **frosted glass** (`bg-black/45 + backdrop-blur-2xl`) so the gradient blurs through them — the effect you asked for.

**Stars around the card** — a deterministic 64-star field in the padded zone around the frame (same seeded approach as the reference's reactive stars; a few twinkle on a slow cycle). Deterministic means server and client render identically — no hydration flicker.

**macOS-style menu bar** — capping the card like the reference, but honest to your product: **NoteFlow** (bold, frontmost app) · File · Edit · View · Meeting · Window · Help on the left; drawn wifi + battery glyphs and "Fri Sep 26 9:41 AM" on the right, at the reference's muted opacity, blurred over the gradient.

**Every scene animates like a screen recording:**
- **Capture** — the Meet URL *types itself* character-by-character with a blinking caret → the button presses → status walks joining → **recording** (pulsing orange) → **ready** ✓
- **Transcript** — the waveform *plays* (bars brighten with the playhead), speaker lines highlight in sync, the timestamp follows
- **Summary** — the recap bullets *stream in* like they're being written, then the action items slide up one after another
- **Ask** — the question types in the input → sends as a bubble → thinking dots → the answer *streams* → the timestamp citation pops in
- **Share** — a **demo cursor glides across the panel** to Copy link, presses it, and the button flips to "✓ Copied"

**Reel mechanics** — scenes auto-advance on their own durations (5.6–8.6s each, matched to their animations), the **active dock item lifts and scales** as the playhead moves, hovering pauses the reel, clicking a dock item jumps to that scene. Reduced-motion visitors get finished frames and manual switching — no autoplay, per your no-overanimation rules' spirit (motion here demonstrates the product, which is the one purpose that earns it).

I verified it live across multiple frames — caught the URL mid-type, the transcript mid-playback, and the cursor resting on "Copied". Once the deploy confirms, watch a full loop at **https://noteflow--ai.vercel.app/product**. Tell me if any scene's pacing feels off (each duration is one number to tune), or if the gradient should be brighter/dimmer behind the glass.

timestamp: 2026-09-25T15:15:45.359Z

Pushed — deploy check running. All three issues addressed:

## 1. Why the glow wasn't showing — and the fix

Two compounding problems: the glow layer was painted **after the stars** (covering them), and more importantly, your gradient image is **mostly dark metallic** — at 50% opacity, blurred, over a pure-black page, dark pixels are simply invisible. Fixed by reordering it beneath the stars, enlarging the bleed (`-inset-8` → `-inset-14` on desktop), and lifting it with `blur(52px) saturate(1.5) brightness(1.6)` at 80% opacity — now the wallpaper's light visibly washes around the desktop card, with the stars twinkling over it.

## 2. Slide transitions

Scenes now move like a reel instead of crossfading in place: the incoming window **slides in from the right**, the outgoing one **exits to the left**, both easing over 500ms with the reel area clipping the motion. The dock tile lift is synchronized with the slide, so the whole thing reads as one playhead advancing.

## 3. Responsive across all sizes

- Desktop height steps: **520px** (phones) → **600px** (tablets) → **640px** (desktop)
- The window is fluid (`w-full max-w-[560px]`) with truncating text everywhere it can overflow (typed URL, titles, share link)
- The action bar's **"Actions ⌘K"** cluster hides on phones (the primary action + ↵ stays); menu-bar app menus and the date already collapse, leaving just "NoteFlow" + status icons
- The dock (5 tiles ≈ 260px) and tooltip fit every width
- Also caught a real bug while in there: two `duration-400` classes — which don't exist in Tailwind's scale, so those transitions were silently running at the 150ms default — corrected to `duration-500`

Verified live mid-animation (caught Ask streaming its answer with the tooltipped dock tile lifted). Once the deploy confirms, check **https://noteflow--ai.vercel.app/product** — if you want the glow even stronger, it's one brightness number to turn up.
